### 5bd · New bug from PageTransition: navigating to a shorter page landed on its footer, not its top — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "it even got wrong, when i go to leggings, it is stuck on the footer." Reproduced immediately and precisely: scrolled home to its own max scroll (~13294px), clicked through to `/activewear/leggings` (a shorter page, max scroll ~7083px), and the new page landed at `scrollY: 7083.5` — exactly that page's own max scroll, i.e. the browser had kept the *old* raw scroll offset across the navigation and simply clamped it to whatever the new, shorter document allowed, instead of resetting to the top the way a normal route change should.

Root cause: `PageTransition`'s `<ViewTransition>` wrapper (5's own page-fade work) means Next's built-in scroll-to-top-on-navigation logic (in its own `layout-router.js`) isn't reliably running. Confirmed live, step by step: called `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` manually after such a navigation and it had *zero effect*, even seconds later — not a timing race, the plain call simply wasn't landing. Switching to `window.scrollTo({top: 0, left: 0, behavior: "instant"})` (overriding this site's own global `scroll-behavior: smooth`) fixed it when called manually, which is what pointed at the real fix.

New `components/ScrollReset.tsx` (client component, rendered once in `app/layout.tsx` next to the existing `AppEntryMarker` — same "small, side-effect-only, mounted once in the root layout" shape): a `useLayoutEffect` keyed on `usePathname()` calls that same instant `scrollTo` on every pathname change — `useLayoutEffect`, not `useEffect`, because the deferred timing of a plain effect left the same window where the stale scroll offset gets clamped before the reset can run. Deliberately skips the very first render (initial page load shouldn't force a scroll) and skips browser back/forward navigations (tracked via a `popstate` listener) — those already get the browser's own correct native scroll restoration, which this must not fight.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live-reproduced the exact failing scenario from the bug report (scroll home to max, click through to leggings) and confirmed `scrollY: 0` on arrival, where it previously read `7083.5`. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

### 5bh · Category page's Footer was bleeding through at the very top of the page — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "when i go to leggings page, it still shows th fixed footer." Reproduced immediately: loading `/activewear/leggings` showed the full site footer (logo, nav columns, "Get in touch," copyright) right underneath the breadcrumb, before any of the page's own real content (CategoryBanner's trust bullets, the product grid, etc.) — not a scroll-position issue at all, a real rendering bug, unrelated to 5bg's scroll fix.

Root cause: `Footer.tsx`'s own header comment spells out its "reveal" trick exactly — the footer is `sticky bottom-0 z-0`, which pins it to the viewport's bottom edge for the *entire* scroll range by itself; the only thing that makes it look like it's hidden until the real end of the page is `<main>` carrying `relative z-10` plus an opaque background, so every section's own background keeps covering the footer during normal scrolling. `app/page.tsx`'s own `<main>` already has `relative z-10 bg-paper ...` for exactly this reason. The category page template's `<main>` had neither class at all — so its footer had nothing covering it, and sat visible at the sticky position closest to the top of the (still mostly empty, still-rendering) page.

Fixed by adding `className="relative z-10 bg-paper"` to `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`'s `<main>`, matching the homepage's own `<main>` exactly (that page's own extra drop-shadow class wasn't copied — no Figma/owner reason for it here, this was purely about restoring the z-index/background relationship the footer's reveal trick actually depends on).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings`: fresh load now shows the real page content (banner, trust bullets, product grid) immediately below the breadcrumb, no footer content visible until scrolled there; scrolling to the real bottom (`scrollY: 7074` of `7974` total) confirmed the footer's own text ("Get in touch") is present and visible at that point, i.e. the reveal trick works correctly again. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing. `npm run build` clean.

**CategoryBanner** ("fix the nav"): at mobile widths, `categoryBanner.section` was an unconditional fixed `h-[340px]` with `contentWrap` unconditionally `absolute`-positioned. Fixed structurally, not by shrinking any type size (no mobile Figma frame exists for this section, so inventing a smaller H1 would have been a real design guess, not a bug fix): below `xl`, `section` is `h-auto` with real padding and `breadcrumbWrap`/`contentWrap` are `static` (normal flow, box grows to fit real content); at `xl` the original fixed-height/absolute-centred desktop layout is untouched.

**FabricOptions** ("fix fabric options text alignment"): row 1's three cells are real `<th scope="col">` elements (from the SEO/AEO finalization pass, 5be) — the browser's own UA stylesheet centre-aligns `<th>` by default — while every row below uses plain `<td>`s, left-aligned by default. With no explicit alignment anywhere in the recipe, row 1 alone rendered centred against every other row's left alignment. Fixed with one `text-left` on `fabricOptions.headerRow`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings` at 375×812: CategoryBanner's content now grows with the box, clean break to the white section below, no overlap. At 1440×900: banner height still measures exactly `340px` (unchanged), and the fabric table's header row now computes `text-align: left`, matching the data rows. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30). `npm run build` clean.

### 5bk · The real "nav" bug: Header's non-sticky variant painting behind Footer's reveal trick — 2026-08-30
Owner report, after 5bj: "nav still is wront, please check", followed by a screenshot showing the header cut off (logo sliced in half, a stray white band above it) at the top of `/activewear/leggings`. 5bj's CategoryBanner fix was correct and re-verified (clean at every supported viewport, 360px–1920px) but did not address this — this was a completely different, genuinely new bug, only visible at certain viewport *heights* (e.g. 1280×720), which is why it hadn't shown up in the width-only Playwright suite or in the live Browser-pane tool's own screenshots (both of which happened to land on unaffected heights, plus that tool's own repeat flakiness this session made it look like another false alarm at first).

Root cause, found by comparing `elementFromPoint` at the very top of the viewport against `closest('header')`/`closest('footer')`: the point resolved inside `<footer>`, not `<header>`. `Footer`'s own `sticky bottom-0 z-0` reveal trick (see its own header comment, and 5bh above) computes as pinned near the viewport's top edge at scroll position 0 on *every* page, confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect` on both this page and the homepage — that part is by design, always covered by `<main>`'s own `relative z-10`. What's specific to this page: its `Header` renders with `sticky={false}` (the "gymshark trial", 2026-08-29), which uses `header.baseStatic` — plain `position: static`, no z-index at all. A `static` element paints in an *earlier* CSS stacking bucket than any positioned one, regardless of z-index value, so this header was painting BEHIND Footer's positioned (`z-0`) box wherever they overlapped — the top ~87px of the page. The homepage's own header (`sticky z-40`) never showed this, since a positioned element with a positive z-index always paints after Footer's z-0 one; only this page's plain-`static` variant was exposed.

Fixed with one class change: `header.baseStatic` gained `relative z-40` (`components/ui/styles.ts`) — `relative` (not `sticky`) keeps this variant's own non-sticky, scroll-away layout completely unchanged; it only opts the element into normal stacking-context ordering so its own `z-40` (matching the sticky header's own value) can do its job. Confirmed via a fresh Playwright check (not the live Browser-pane tool, given its repeat flakiness this session) that `elementFromPoint` at the top of the viewport now resolves inside `<header>`, at both 1280×720 (the exact reported viewport) and the previously-working 375×812/1440×900, and that Footer's own reveal-at-the-real-bottom mechanic (5bh) still works correctly on both.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30), including 1280×720. `npm run build` clean.

### 5bq · TextReveal: real bug, an embedded "\n" scrambled word order instead of breaking the line — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "fabric section title should be in 2 lines." It was rendering 3 lines, and once past that, the words after the break rendered out of order ("The fabrics behind the brands" / "big" instead of "The fabrics behind the" / "big brands").

Two layered causes, both fixed:
- **Container too narrow**: `FabricOptions`' `headingBlock` was `max-w-[579px]` at every breakpoint (matching `WhatWeCover`'s own centred column, copied without re-checking against this heading's own longer first line). At 579px, even "The fabrics behind the" alone wrapped a second time. Widened to `xl:max-w-[750px]` (measured live: the minimum width that first line fits on one line at this heading's 54px desktop size; 700px still wrapped, 750px didn't). Mobile kept its own confirmed 579px, unaffected.
- **The real bug**: `FabricOptions` renders its heading through `TextReveal` (the word-by-word entrance animation), and `TextReveal.tsx` split words with a bare `text.split(" ")` — a `"\n"` embedded in `Category.fabricHeading` doesn't contain a space, so `"the\nbig"` survived as one single "word" token, embedded whole inside that word's own `overflow-hidden` animated mask span. That's not merely "failed to break" — an embedded newline inside a masked, animated inline-block scrambled the actual rendered line order, which is why raising the container width alone wasn't enough. Fixed in `TextReveal.tsx` itself: split on `"\n"` first into lines, render a real `<br />` between them, then split each line into words as before (reveal-index keeps incrementing globally across the break, so the stagger animation is unaffected). This is a shared, sitewide component — the fix works for any future heading that needs a forced line break through `TextReveal`, not just this one string. `fabricOptions.heading` lost the `whitespace-pre-line`/`text-wrap` overrides tried first (the real fix made them unnecessary — the break is a real `<br />` now, not a literal text character needing special CSS handling).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport permanent Playwright suite passing (`TextReveal` is used sitewide — Hero and every other reveal heading, none of which use embedded newlines, are confirmed unaffected). Live-checked: rendered DOM now shows a real `<br>` between "the" and "big" with each word in its own clean, unmodified mask; heading height computes to exactly 2 lines at both mobile (`68px` / `34px` line-height) and desktop (`128px` / `64px` line-height).

### 5bv · Section divider: stale 120px margin corrected to 72px, no double-stacking with WhatWeCover's own spacing — 2026-08-30
Owner: "what we cover should have 72px gap from the top of heading to the top section, there is extra space remove everything unecessary." Live-measured before this fix: mobile had `232px` from the divider to the heading, desktop `174px` — both bloated by the same root cause.

The page-level divider's own `mb-[120px]` was a flat, unconditional value written back when `WhatWeCover` was the LAST of the 3 sections (pre-5as reorder), stacking with whatever came after it. Once `WhatWeCover` moved to be FIRST (5as), its own `section` recipe grew a real, Figma-confirmed `py-[72px]` on mobile specifically so it could supply its own top spacing — but the divider's stale `mb-[120px]` kept firing too, doubling mobile's gap (`120` divider + `72` own padding = `192` before the eyebrow/heading gap on top of that). Desktop's `WhatWeCover` has no top padding of its own by design (relies entirely on this divider), so its own `120px` was simply the wrong number post-reorder, never re-checked against the new `72px` target.

Fixed: divider margin is now `xl:mb-[72px]` with none below `xl` — mobile relies purely on `WhatWeCover`'s own confirmed `72px` section padding (no stacking), desktop gets the requested `72px` divider gap directly since that's its only spacing source.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Full 30-viewport permanent Playwright suite passing. ### 5bw · FabricOptions mobile accordion: real transition-snap + title-shift bug fixed — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "The transition of opening and closing the container should be very smooth. Also, when I expand the content, the title moves a little, nothing should move from its position." Both were real bugs in `5bp`'s first build, not tuning:

1. **No animation at all, just a snap.** The first build branched to two entirely different markup shapes per row — an open `<div>` with its detail block mounted straight at `grid-rows-[1fr]`, or a closed `<button>` with no detail block in the DOM whatsoever. A CSS transition can't animate a value into existence on first mount, and can't animate a value out on unmount either — so opening/closing just snapped. Fixed by mounting the SAME structure (header + detail block) for every row always, exactly like `CategoryFilters`' own groups, toggling only the grid track class (`accordionDetailGrid` 0fr ↔ `accordionDetailGridOpen` 1fr) — now there's a real starting frame for the transition to animate from/to.
2. **Title shifting 1px on toggle.** The closed row had `border border-[#e8ecf1]` (1px) and the open row had no border property at all — under `border-box` sizing, adding/removing a border shifts the interior content box by the border's own width, nudging the title. Fixed by keeping a `border` (1px) present at BOTH states on a new shared `accordionItem` wrapper class, only ever toggling its *colour* (`accordionItemOpen`'s `border-transparent` vs `accordionItemClosed`'s `border-[#e8ecf1]`) — identical box geometry either way, so the title never moves. Also added `transition-colors duration-300` to that same wrapper so the bg/border swap itself animates too, not just the height, matching "very smooth."

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Live on `/activewear/leggings` at 375px: title's `getBoundingClientRect()` top/left measured identical before and after collapsing the open row (no shift). `document.querySelectorAll('...').getAnimations()` on the toggled wrapper confirmed a real `CSSTransition` running with the correct keyframes (`border-color`/`background-color` interpolating to their real target values) — could not observe the animation's live frame-by-frame progress in this session's automated browser tab, since it reports `document.visibilityState: "hidden"` and Chrome freezes the compositor clock (rAF, CSS transitions) for backgrounded tabs; the keyframe data itself is the correctness proof, and the underlying technique is identical to `CategoryFilters`' own already-shipped, working accordion. Full 30-viewport `tests/screenshots.spec.ts` suite re-run against the fixed build.

On mobile, `FinalCta`'s ticker block (the compliance ticker, titled "STANDARD ON EVERY ORDER") is the first thing in that section — on the Activewear PLP it sits directly below `Faq`. `Faq`'s own `mobileSection` already carries the sitewide standard `pb-[72px]` ([02-design-system.md](02-design-system.md)'s documented mobile section-to-section rhythm); `finalCta.mobileTickerBlock` also carried its own `pt-[72px]`, stacking to a real 144px gap.

Superseded 5bu's just-shipped mobile-subline split on `ProductCard` (which had shortened, not removed, the per-tile text): `ProductCard`'s subline `<p>` is now desktop-only (`max-xl:hidden`, no mobile counterpart at all) — the "in custom fabrics & colors" detail moved up to `CategoryMetaStrip` instead, said once above the whole grid. New `Category.gridSublineMobile` field (`content/activewear/types.ts`, populated in `leggings.ts`) renders through a new `categorySublineMobile` prop on `CategoryMetaStrip`, alongside the existing desktop `categorySubline` — same "two `<p>`s, one hidden per breakpoint" pattern `ProductCard` used before this change, since the two sentences genuinely differ (not a CSS truncation of one string). `app/styleguide/page.tsx`'s own `CategoryMetaStrip` call updated to pass the new required prop.

**Second**, after that landed: "keep the product title to 1 line. gap of pagination from the top of the product should be 40px. gap from pagination and the below separator should be 24px. Pagination previous button icon should be left to the previous text." — four corrections to 5bu's own just-shipped work:
- `productCard.title` reverted from a 2-line `min-h-[34px]` reservation back to `max-xl:truncate` (1 line, ellipsis on overflow) — the owner's very next message reversed their own prior "should have 2 lines" request from earlier the same day.
- `pagination.row`'s own `py-12` (48px) had been the *only* source of both the grid-to-pagination and pagination-to-divider gaps, at every breakpoint. Split to `max-xl:pt-10 max-xl:pb-0 xl:py-12` — 40px above at mobile, and *no* bottom padding of its own there, since the page wrapper below `ProductGrid` already supplies its own confirmed 24px bottom gap (`app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`'s `max-xl:pb-6`, from 5bu) down to the section divider; `row` having a second bottom padding too would have doubled that 24px into 72px total (confirmed live this was exactly the bug: measured `72px`, not `24px`, before this fix).
- `Pagination.tsx`'s "Previous" button: the "‹" character baked into the same text node as "Previous" replaced with a real `ChevronArrowIcon` element (MobileNav's own reusable left-pointing chevron, unrotated) rendered before the text inside `edgeCell`'s existing flex row — a discrete element in flex order can't render out of place the way a text glyph's own visual weight/alignment could read as "not really to the left." New `pagination.edgeIcon` recipe entry. `Next`'s own "›" stays a text glyph, not flagged.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean after both rounds. Fresh Playwright checks on `/activewear/leggings` at 375×812: mobile page subline renders `"Every style is available in custom fabrics & colors."`, product tiles show no subline paragraph at all (only the desktop one exists in the DOM, `display: none` below `xl`); card title computes `text-overflow: ellipsis`/`white-space: nowrap`; gap from the last product row to the first pagination button is exactly `40px`, gap from pagination's own bottom to the divider is exactly `24px`; the Previous button's `<svg>` is confirmed its first child. At 1440×900: card title `text-overflow: clip`/`white-space: normal` (unaffected), desktop subline/copy unchanged. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing after each round. `npm run build` clean.

### 5bz · Real pagination bug: fixed "Previous" width let it overflow into the next cell — 2026-08-30
Owner report: "previous box is touching the other box, no spacing in between." First checked whether the row's own `gap-2` was actually applying — confirmed live at both 375px and the owner's own requested 360px width that it was, with a zoomed screenshot showing a clean 8px gap in both cases, so reported back that it couldn't be reproduced and asked for a screenshot. The owner's screenshot showed the real thing: "Previous"'s own text visibly ran edge-to-edge inside its box with no right-side padding, touching the orange "1" cell directly.

Root cause: `pagination.edgeCell` had `max-xl:w-[80px]`, a hard fixed width — but flex items default to `min-width: auto`, meaning a flex item never shrinks below its own content's minimum intrinsic size even when an explicit smaller `width` is set. The icon+gap+"Previous" content, once its own `px-5` padding is counted, genuinely needed more room than 80px could give it (confirmed live: real content measured `~107px`). Whenever font metrics pushed the content past that threshold (browser/device-dependent — this is exactly why it didn't reproduce in every test render), the button rendered wider than its own declared 80px, overflowing straight into the gap meant for the next cell.

Fixed by dropping the fixed width entirely: `max-xl:w-fit` (plus `whitespace-nowrap`) sizes the box to its real content instead, so it can never overflow itself regardless of font rendering. Desktop was never affected (no width class of its own at `xl`).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. Fresh Playwright checks at 360px: "Previous" now measures its real content width (`~107px`, not the old fixed `80px`), with the row's own `8px` gap to "1" confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect` and a zoomed screenshot. Full 30-viewport Playwright suite passing (30/30). `npm run build` clean.

### 5ce · Internal review before locking the master PLP template: full mobile pass + SEO/AEO/GEO audit — 2026-08-30
Owner request: "Let's go through the entire page mobile and see if we are all good to ship. Also make sure we cover everything from SEO AEO AND GEO pov. Do you internal review and lock this page. We will build lot other category pages based on this master PLP template."

**One real bug found and fixed**: the mobile Filter FAB (`CategoryFilters.tsx`, 5bt) was plain `position: fixed` with no visibility scoping at all — it stayed pinned to the viewport for the entire page's scroll range, not just the filters+grid area. Live-checked scrolling the full mobile page: it floated over `WhatWeCover`'s own body copy ("Branding" text partly unreadable), `TrustPoints`, `FabricOptions`' eyebrow, FAQ answers, and the Footer's own LinkedIn icon (a real, tappable link partly covered). Fixed with an `IntersectionObserver` on a new `#plp-listing` anchor (the filters+grid row itself, `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`) — the FAB now shows only while that row is actually in view, confirmed via a scripted scroll check (visible at `y=0/800`, correctly hidden from `y=1900` onward through the Footer).

**Full mobile pass, real device widths (360/375/390/412/430)**: zero horizontal overflow at any of them, zero console errors, zero failed network requests, exactly one `<h1>` and one `<main>`. Section-by-section screenshot review (banner → meta strip/grid → pagination → WhatWeCover → TrustPoints → FabricOptions → FAQ → FinalCta → Footer) found nothing else visually wrong after the FAB fix.

**SEO/AEO/GEO audit against `docs/06-seo.md`'s own checklist**, all confirmed live and in the real `next build` static output (not just dev mode):
- Metadata: title/description/canonical/OG/Twitter all present, all resolving to the real `https://www.capriosports.com/capriowear/...` production domain, not this app's own internal routing root.
- 5 JSON-LD blocks render correctly: `Organization`+`WebSite` (sitewide, from `app/layout.tsx`), `BreadcrumbList`, `CollectionPage` (a real `ItemList` of all 9 style cards), `FAQPage` (all 11 real Q&A pairs) — every one fed by the same data the visible page renders, none hand-typed twice.
- Heading hierarchy: exactly one H1 (`data.h1`), correct H2 → H3 nesting throughout (`CategoryMetaStrip`'s H2 → `ProductCard`'s H3 tiles; each section's own H2 → the FAQ's own H3 questions) — no skipped levels anywhere.
- 3 `<nav>` landmarks, each with its own distinguishing `aria-label` ("Main", "Breadcrumb", "Activewear categories") — no ambiguity for a screen reader.
- Real semantic HTML confirmed throughout, not styled divs: `FabricOptions` uses a real `<table>`, `WhatWeCover`/`RelatedCategories` use real `<ul>/<li>`, every internal link is a real `<a>` (`next/link`/`Button`).
- `RelatedCategories` (SEO rule 6, internal linking in real body copy) is wired and live, real labels (no leftover `PLACEHOLDER:` text) — confirmed nothing user-facing anywhere on the page still carries that prefix.
- `app/sitemap.ts` already includes this route automatically (reads the same `categories` registry the page itself reads from — a new category file is in the sitemap the moment it's added to that registry, no second list to maintain). `app/robots.ts` doesn't block it.
- Alt text: no real photography yet anywhere on this page (`MediaPlaceholder` boxes only), but `imageAlt`/`label` wiring is already correct and future-proof — a real image dropped into any `image` field renders with real alt text automatically, no separate pass needed later.
- Content rules: no "seamless", no en/em dashes, no prices, American spelling, confirmed via a direct grep of `leggings.ts`.
- `npm run build` clean; `/activewear/leggings` confirmed statically prerendered (SSG via `generateStaticParams`), not server-rendered on demand — fast and fully crawlable.

**One open decision, not fixed silently**: `Category.overview` and `Category.specQuickFacts` are both fully typed and filled in `leggings.ts`, both still carry the `PLACEHOLDER:` prefix, and **neither is wired into the page anywhere** — confirmed via a direct grep of `app/activewear/[category]/page.tsx`. No leak risk (unrendered data can't show placeholder text to a user or a crawler), but every future category file built from this "master template" will still need to fill in two fields nothing currently renders. Worth an explicit call before treating the shape as final: build the two remaining sections now, or drop/mark them optional until they're actually needed.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint .` clean. `npm run build` clean. Full 30-viewport permanent Playwright suite passing (this route stays outside it per 5ag's own note, verified separately via scripted mobile/desktop checks instead).

5ce's review flagged one open decision (build the two remaining unwired sections, or drop the requirement) with no owner response yet — resolved to the lower-risk default rather than expanding scope with two new unrequested sections. `Category.overview` and `Category.specQuickFacts` (`content/activewear/types.ts`) are now optional (`?:`), each with a doc comment explaining they're unwired and should be filled in only once a real section actually renders them. `leggings.ts`'s own placeholder values are left as-is (harmless, unrendered) rather than deleted, so nothing is lost if those sections get built later.

## Decision log

Decisions worth not re-litigating. Newest first.

| Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-26 | Hero's `.hero-wide-pilot` (1680px at 1920px+) removed; every section, Hero included, stays at the standard 1440px `container-p` cap, no exceptions | Superseded the 2026-08-23 per-section opt-in below — confirmed on a real 1920px screen that Hero reading visibly wider than every other section looked like an inconsistency, not a deliberate wide moment. The class is deleted outright (`app/globals.css`, `components/ui/styles.ts`), not just unused |
| 2026-08-26 | A heading inside a flex column that gets narrower under `flex-shrink` (no `shrink-0`) cannot be fixed by raising the heading's own `max-width` — the column's rendered width is set by the flex algorithm, not by the child's CSS | Confirmed by live measurement on Our Services' sticky heading: setting `max-width: 2000px` inline didn't move its rendered width at all, which stayed exactly `1280 - gap - card width` (579px) at every desktop viewport. The real fix for a heading that only breaks at 1920px+ (text-h1's own clamp growing past what a fixed-width column has room for) is freezing that heading's font-size at its 1440 value via `min-\[1920px\]:!text-[...]`, not touching the column's width |
| 2026-08-26 | An arbitrary `min-[Npx]:` variant competing with an existing `xl:`-scoped utility for the same CSS property needs `!important` to reliably win, not just a later position in the class string | Confirmed live: `min-[1920px]:text-[3.375rem]` compiled correctly and appeared in the class list, but `xl:text-h1` (from a shared, earlier-applied recipe) still won at a real 1920px viewport — the same class of same-specificity cascade-order bug already documented for `max-xl:`/`xl:` pairs, but here the two variants aren't mutually exclusive ranges (1920px is a subset of `xl`'s range), so `!important` is the correct fix, not a reordering |
| 2026-08-24 | An eyebrow paired with a heading is always `#ABB5C0` on a dark section, always `#17191E` on a light one, sitewide, no exceptions — `Eyebrow`'s `tone` prop is required, no default | Owner call, explicitly confirmed to include Hero's eyebrow even though that overrides previously-verified accent-orange styling — worth confirming rather than guessing given the cost of wrongly undoing already-correct work. A required prop with no fallback means no future caller can silently inherit the wrong colour |
| 2026-08-24 | A carousel card that must resize based on which one is "active" (nearest scroll-snap centre) swaps its `MediaPlaceholder` `ratio` at a fixed width, rather than setting an explicit height directly | The resize is then driven entirely by `aspect-ratio`, so there's no separate height value to keep in sync with which card is active — confirmed as the right approach after finding Inside the Factory's real Figma geometry has the active card genuinely taller (300×340) than its neighbours (300×248), not a uniform size merely clipped by the viewport |
| 2026-08-24 | Inside the Factory's mobile gallery is a manual finger-swipe carousel (native CSS scroll-snap), not auto-rotating and not custom drag-tracking JS | Owner corrected an initial misreading of their animation reference (a Shopify page) — they were swiping by hand, the finger just wasn't visible in the recording. Native scroll-snap gives smooth drag-following and snap-to-centre behaviour for free, at any mobile viewport width |
| 2026-08-24 | A fixed-width flex row that must fit multiple desktop viewports never uses `shrink-0` on its children | Confirmed as a real bug: Inside the Factory's 3-tile gallery (1436px combined) overflowed at 1280px/1366px because `shrink-0` prevented the tiles from narrowing. Default `flex-shrink: 1` lets tiles shrink together, proportionally, so the row always fits without losing its ratio |
| 2026-08-24 | A section's full-bleed gallery/media block is a sibling of its `container-p`-padded heading, not nested inside it, whenever Figma shows the gallery spanning the full frame width | Confirmed via `get_metadata` on Inside the Factory: the gallery's real x-position spans the frame's full width (0 to 1440), unlike the heading above it, which does use the standard 80px inset — nesting the gallery inside the heading's padded wrapper would have constrained it to the wrong width |
| 2026-08-24 | A full-bleed section background (`bg-ink` or similar) always lives on its own unconstrained wrapper, never on the same element as `container-p` | `container-p` caps at `max-width: 1440px`, so a background on that same element stops at 1440px too, showing the page's default background outside it on any wider viewport instead of true edge-to-edge colour. Confirmed as a real bug on Stats' desktop background; Hero already had this right by splitting outer background from inner `container-p`, which is now the standing pattern every full-bleed section should follow |
| 2026-08-24 | Stats' second stat ("75,000 sq ft" vs "50,000 sq ft") follows the desktop Figma frame on both breakpoints | Figma's own desktop and mobile frames genuinely disagree; asked the owner directly rather than picking one, since this was already flagged as an unresolved discrepancy before the section was even built |
| 2026-08-24 | A rounded border-collapse grid puts the entire outer border on one element (the grid itself), never split across a wrapper and its cells via edge-ownership + a clipping `overflow-hidden` mask | Confirmed via an owner screenshot that the clip approach produces a visibly jagged corner, not a smooth curve — a border and its `border-radius` only render natively smooth when they live on the same element. Cells draw only their internal dividers, never an outer edge |
| 2026-08-24 | A section with its own full-bleed background colour (currently only Hero) gets the standard section-to-section gap as a `margin` on the section itself, not `padding` on its last inner element | Padding sits inside the element's own background box, so it renders as more of that colour, not a visible gap — confirmed as a real bug on Hero's dark ticker. Margin sits outside the box, showing the page's own background instead. Content inside such a box keeps a separate, fixed 48px inset from the box's own edges regardless of this |
| 2026-08-24 | Every mobile section uses the same pattern for the gap to the next section: `pt-0`, gap contributed entirely by `pb-[72px]` — not two independently-set top/bottom values per section | Measuring the actual gaps showed four different values (88/40/128/180px), each a correct answer to a different question at a different time, not a real system. A single standard, always produced the same way, means any future section's gap is right by construction |
| 2026-08-24 | Section components take content as props (`typeof home.x`), not a direct `content/home.ts` import; not yet promoted further into a fully generic, schema-driven layout system | Prop-ifying makes a section's layout reusable on another page (pass different content, no code change) at low, reversible cost. A fully generic system would mean designing an API for patterns only used once so far — better decided once a second real page actually needs one, not guessed at now |
| 2026-08-24 | Every certification logo gets its own separate mobile export (`mobileSrc`), never the desktop file reused at the mobile box's dimensions | Confirmed real: BSCI and IMAC crop to a genuinely different aspect ratio on mobile than on desktop, so displaying the desktop file at mobile's box size stretched the artwork even after the box's own dimensions were fixed. An inline-style dimension fix solves the box, not what's inside it |
| 2026-08-23 | An image whose displayed aspect ratio differs from its source file's own intrinsic ratio gets an explicit inline `style` width/height, not just the `width`/`height` props | Tailwind's own preflight sets `img { height: auto }`, which silently overrides `next/image`'s height attribute whenever those two ratios differ (confirmed on Certified & Compliant's mobile logos). Inline style always wins over any stylesheet rule, so it's the only reliable fix — a class-based one would just be a different specificity guess |
| 2026-08-23 | A flex-column child that must fill the full available width (e.g. a grid wrapper) always gets an explicit `w-full`, never left to size by content | Confirmed live: `items-center` on a flex column makes children shrink to their own content width by default, not stretch — Certified & Compliant's mobile grid sat 23px further in than intended because of this, easy to miss since it doesn't cause any layout error, just an incorrect inset |
| 2026-08-23 | Images far enough down the homepage get `priority` (eager load) instead of relying on `next/image`'s default lazy load | Confirmed live that Certified & Compliant's logos (the furthest-down real images on the site so far) never loaded via default lazy loading, even scrolled fully into view — silent, no console error, broke the Playwright screenshot QA gate. A handful of small images loading eagerly is a reasonable trade for deterministic rendering |
| 2026-08-23 | `SectionHeading` gained a real `align` prop (`"left"` default, `"center"`) instead of a one-off centred markup for Certified & Compliant | Confirmed via Figma that this section's heading is genuinely centred (Trust Signals and What We Make are genuinely left-aligned) — a real, reusable variant other sections may need later, not a single-use hack |
| 2026-08-23 | Certified & Compliant's mobile grid corner rounding is one `rounded-[4px] overflow-hidden` wrapper around the whole grid, not per-cell corner classes matching Figma's literal per-cell structure | Figma rounds only the 4 true-corner cells individually, but that doesn't compose with the border-collapse technique already in use (a corner's curve would need to span two different elements' borders, wrapper and cell). One rounded, clipped outer wrapper gets the identical visual result without that conflict |
| 2026-08-23 | A heading's mobile-only type overrides always use `max-xl:` (not a bare, unprefixed utility) paired with `xl:` for the desktop/token value | Confirmed live that a bare mobile utility can still win the cascade at desktop widths against a same-specificity `xl:` utility touching the same property -- Tailwind doesn't guarantee source order settles it the way the class-list order suggests. Fixed in `sectionHeading.heading` and retroactively in `hero.eyebrowSize`, which had shipped with the same unsafe shape |
| 2026-08-23 | Overriding a compound type token's *size* alone must also re-set whatever else that token bundles (weight, in Overline's case), not just the size | `hero.eyebrowSize` had overridden Overline's size for Hero's mobile eyebrow without re-setting its paired 600 weight, so it silently rendered at the browser default (400) since Hero shipped, undetected until What We Make's identical pattern was checked and this one was audited too |
| 2026-08-23 | `SectionHeading` and `Card`'s recipe guesses (gap values, mobile heading weight/line-height) corrected on first real use, not left as-is | Both shipped before any real section used them, so their numbers were reasonable placeholders. What We Make is the first real Figma frame for either, so their gaps and mobile-only discrepancies get corrected now rather than carried forward as silent inaccuracies |
| 2026-08-23 | Container widening at 1920px+ (1440 to 1680) stays a per-section opt-in (`.hero-wide-pilot`, Hero only), not the shared `--container-page` token | Tried sitewide first; reverted the same day after owner review — reads well on Hero (video block, short heading) but spreads Client Logos and Trust Signals' grid/text content too thin. Widening is a visual judgement call per section, not a default every `.container-p` user should inherit |
| 2026-08-24 | `MediaPlaceholder`'s light-tone placeholder has no border at all, sitewide | Owner call, explicitly "never applies to any section moving forward" — this is a shared-recipe change (`media.placeholderLight`), not scoped to the section being worked on when it was raised |
| 2026-08-23 | Figma's stray "⚠️" marks on two Trust Signals bodies are left out of displayed copy, not rendered | They read as the designer's own "needs verification" flag on wording, the same convention this project's own `// VERIFY` comments use, not real end-user copy. The two affected items are marked `// VERIFY` in `content/home.ts` |
| 2026-08-23 | `content/home.ts`'s `trustStrip[].body` became a segment array (`string \| { bold: string }`) instead of a plain string | Two of the four Trust Signals items carry a real inline bold span in Figma's own copy; a segment array covers that without building a full rich-text system, since no other section body has needed emphasis before |
| 2026-08-23 | `MediaPlaceholder`'s ratio options extended with two exact, non-rounded ratios (`50:37`, `16:11`) for Trust Signals' media boxes | Both boxes (400×296px desktop, 320×220px mobile) are landscape and don't match any of the app's existing `16:9`/`4:5`/`1:1` options — `4:5` in particular would have been flatly wrong, since it's portrait. Kept exact rather than rounded to an existing ratio |
| 2026-08-24 | Adjacent-cell borders use the edge-ownership technique (each cell draws only its own right/bottom; a wrapper draws the outer top/left), not `gap` or `outline` | `gap` cannot go negative under any circumstances (CSS spec, not a browser quirk); `outline` still belongs to one element at a time, so two adjacent outlines double up exactly like two borders do. Edge-ownership is the only one of the three that makes doubling structurally impossible |
| 2026-08-24 | Registry `width`/`height` always mean the node's exact Figma export size, never a source file's own intrinsic size | The two can differ (a higher-res substitute file has a slightly different aspect ratio than the tight Figma crop) — using the export size as the single meaning avoids silent distortion |
| 2026-08-24 | A single brand can have two different real assets for two different contexts (`mobileOverride`), rather than always reusing one image everywhere | Confirmed real: WOLFpak's desktop node is icon-only, its mobile grid fill is icon-plus-wordmark. Forcing one asset into both contexts would misrepresent whichever one Figma didn't actually specify |
| 2026-08-24 | `.claude/launch.json` restored to a real launch command, not attach-only | Attach-only (`url` with no command) meant the preview tooling could reconnect to a running server but never restart one that had stopped — found the hard way when the dev server died mid-session |
| 2026-08-24 | Logo assets exported per-node from Figma (`download_assets` on each individual logo), never from the parent frame's aggregated raw-image list | The aggregate list mixed in unrelated raster fills for at least one logo (RISE is a vector shape in Figma; its "raw image" from the aggregate call was an unrelated texture) — per-node export is the only way to guarantee the correct asset per brand |
| 2026-08-24 | Mobile client logos use a genuine `grid-cols-2`, not `LogoRow` | `LogoRow` is a flowing wrap, which can't guarantee exactly 2 per row at every width the way an explicit grid can. Figma's mobile layout is a fixed 2-column grid |
| 2026-08-24 | `Marquee` gained `separator="none"` and `gap="loose"` as opt-in variants rather than changing its defaults | The client logos ticker has no separator glyph and a wider (72px) gap than the offerings ticker (40px) — both confirmed per-ticker from Figma, not interchangeable, so the existing ticker's behaviour stays untouched |
| 2026-08-23 | Absolutely-positioned fills inside a rounded shell (`MediaPlaceholder`'s placeholder, overlay, image) must carry `rounded-[inherit]` | `overflow-hidden` on the shell only clips content that overflows it — it doesn't round a child's own square corners that already fit inside the box. Without this, a "rounded" placeholder quietly renders square-cornered borders and images |
| 2026-08-23 | `MediaPlaceholder`'s dark-tone border uses `border-paper/20`, not the standard `border-line-dark` hairline (12%) | A structural hairline is the right weight for a header's bottom border; on a near-black placeholder against a near-black page it reads as no border at all |
| 2026-08-23 | `Marquee` defaults to a stacked label-above-ticker layout, not label-beside-ticker | The only ticker read from real Figma so far uses stacked; the inline layout was an unverified guess. Kept as an opt-in `layout="inline"` in case a future ticker wants it |
| 2026-08-22 | Homepage sections built desktop and mobile together, one section at a time, not a full desktop pass then a full mobile pass | Owner's call. Matches the existing "done means every viewport" rule and avoids re-opening already-"done" sections for a second pass later |
| 2026-08-22 | Desktop container padding corrected from 48px to 80px, sitewide | Figma's real measured content margin, confirmed on both the nav and Hero frames. Owner's call after being shown the discrepancy affected already-shipped, already-reviewed work |
| 2026-08-22 | Section compositions live in `components/sections/`, separate from `/components` | A section is a page-specific composition of real copy and layout, not a reusable atom — a different kind of thing from `Button` or `Card`, so it gets its own place rather than diluting `/components` |
| 2026-08-22 | `Marquee` gained `labelVariant` and `separator` props instead of changing its defaults | The real offerings ticker needs a bold non-uppercase label and a sparkle separator, but the compliance ticker (section 10) hasn't had its own Figma read yet — changing the default would be guessing for a section not yet designed |
| 2026-08-22 | Mobile offerings ticker is a plain vertical list, not `Marquee` in a mobile mode | Confirmed against the real mobile Figma frame: no scrolling, no icons, structurally a different layout, not a narrower version of the desktop ticker |
| 2026-08-22 | Headline-scale type tokens (Display, H1-4, Stat Number/Label) keep scaling past 1440 up to a new max at 1920; reading/UI styles stay fixed at every width | Owner reported large text looking small on 1920. Figma's frame was 1440 and specifies nothing past it, so extending headline styles isn't a Figma disagreement — it fills a gap Figma left open. Reading text doesn't get the same treatment since scaling it further doesn't aid readability |
| 2026-08-22 | Desktop nav breakpoint moved from a custom 1420px back to the standard `xl` (1280px) | Real Figma nav content (logo, 4 plain links) is much narrower than the wireframe-era placeholder; a standard breakpoint now fits all four required desktop viewports instead of two |
| 2026-08-22 | Nav content is 4 plain links, no mega menu, resolving open question 6 | The real Figma design (node 316:1331) settles it; neither the wireframe's four nor the copy doc's six-plus-mega-menu guess was quite right |
| 2026-08-22 | Logo is an inlined SVG component, not a `/public` image file | Only an inlined SVG can take `currentColor`, matching the design system's established light/dark pattern; an `<img>` cannot |
| 2026-08-22 | Claude defaults to acting on its own recommendation instead of asking first; asks only when there is no confident recommendation, the call is hard to undo, or the consequence needs the owner's say | Owner asked for less gating on routine decisions. Updated rules 2 and 3 in [01-ways-of-working.md](01-ways-of-working.md) |
| 2026-08-22 | Desktop nav switches to the drawer at a custom 1420px breakpoint, not 1024px | At Figma's real type sizes the nav row does not fit in one line any narrower; owner chose the drawer for 1280/1366 over shrinking the header's content further |
| 2026-08-22 | Typography is copied from Figma exactly; colour, spacing and radius are not | Owner asked specifically for type. Figma's neutrals (`#131314`/`#F2F2F7`) and radius scale (4/8/14/100) also differ from ours and remain a separate, undecided question |
| 2026-08-21 | Styling lives in one central recipe file, not inline in components | Restyling the whole site becomes one edit in one place; components cannot drift apart |
| 2026-08-21 | `CLAUDE.md` stays slim; detail lives in numbered `/docs` files | It loads every session, so length costs tokens every time. Separate docs are also easier for a non-developer to read one at a time |
| 2026-08-21 | Retrofitted all thirteen existing components immediately rather than applying the new system only to new ones | Avoids carrying two competing conventions through the rest of the build |
| 2026-08-21 | Nav is built from the copy doc's six links plus mega menus, not the wireframe's four | The copy doc is the copy source of truth. Flagged as open question 6 for confirmation |
| 2026-08-21 | Drawer renders through a portal | The sticky header creates a layer that would otherwise trap the drawer under other content |
| 2026-08-21 | Desktop nav switches to the drawer below 1024px, not 768px | Six links plus two CTAs cannot hold a 44px tap target on one tablet row |
| Earlier | Marquee is CSS-only, with the list rendered twice and moved exactly half its width | Seamless loop at any content width with zero client JavaScript and nothing to measure |
| Earlier | Components that sit on both light and dark sections inherit colour instead of taking an "on dark" prop | One component, two contexts, no duplicated variants |
